(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) I am very pleased that the report on the situation of people with disabilities is being dealt with in plenary, although I am somewhat sad that the debate on the Rules of Procedure has previously limited the time for dealing with this important report, but that is what it is. I am sorry that people with disabilities have deserved themselves all the time. This report gives us the opportunity, on the one hand, to talk about the requirements of people with disabilities, but also on the other hand to show the measures already taken for people with disabilities. At the same time, he also gives us the opportunity to talk about the future challenges. And the report includes – which is also very important – a series of practical information for the professional audience, but also for people with disabilities. (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) People with disabilities are not a heterogeneous group, which is a group with very special needs, with very different demands and also very different requirements. I think the policy for people with disabilities has to be just as diverse. In the end, however, there must always be one thing: participation, inclusion of people with disabilities in social and economic life. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] The efforts of the Federal Government are going exactly in this direction. If you look at the report, you can also see that great progress has been made in the field of disability policy. I only recall the recognition of sign language – a very, very important milestone in the policy for disabled people. I recall the decision of the disability equalisation package and its renewal. I would remind you of the introduction of the disabled lawyer, the Bundespflegegeldsgesetz, which increased the care allowance. And I also recall the ratification of the UN Convention for People with Disabilities, Austria being one of the first countries to ratify this UN Convention. There have also been a number of awareness-raising measures. I am convinced that these awareness-raising measures are as important as legal frameworks. We launched the Year of Equal Opportunities in 2007. I'm convinced that this is all, It is very important to help the people to raise awareness of the problems of people with disabilities, to highlight them and, of course, to improve them. However, I am also convinced that much has been done in this area in recent decades, especially in self-understanding in dealing with people with disabilities. We are far from the goal – I am convinced of that too – but it is a great task for the future to continue to work on improving living conditions for people with disabilities. I have already said that the report also shows us where the challenges of the future lie, and they are very diverse. They start in the field of education. If you look at 38 percent of people with disabilities having only compulsory schooling, you simply need action. There is also much to be done in employment. The employment rate of people with disabilities is almost half lower at 34 percent than in people who are not disabled. This, of course, is also reflected in income. We have already heard from Mr Hofer today that people with disabilities are also affected twice as much by poverty as people who are not disabled. Although the social benefits in Austria significantly reduce the risk of poverty, I am convinced that there is still a great deal of political effort in this area. For me, it is especially important to enable people with disabilities to integrate into the labour market because I am convinced that self-determined life is inseparably linked to work. I am therefore also very happy and grateful that Federal Minister Hundstorfer has clearly promised that there will be no savings in the area of such important labour market policies. In the future, employment projects, clearing jobs, coaching jobs, work assistance, the entrepreneurship service, inclusive vocational training, to name just a few, will continue to be able to do their important work for people with disabilities. I'm really happy about that. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen, I am convinced, however, that we need to do a lot more specifically in the field of awareness raising for entrepreneurs, so that the entrepreneurs realize that disabled people are valuable employees who are powerful, powerful and powerful.

On the one hand, there is the permanent impairment, which is of course the worst, but also the temporary. I just think that there are about 70 000 people who have a temporary leg fracture and have a certain handicap. But the subject of course is the permanent impairment; these are 20.5 percent in Austria, which are constantly more or less restricted in their lives. The impairments are of various kinds. One has a visual impairment, the other has a hearing impairment, there is a physical disability and a mental disability. Considering that 7 percent of the population have an impairment, that is 580 000 people in Austria. 50,000 people are dependent on a wheelchair, ladies and gentlemen. Women with disabilities are more rarely employed than men with disabilities, they receive lower benefits, they receive rare sickness-related pensions, and their average benefit from these pensions is about half as high as those of men. This spiral begins with sickness, disability, unemployment, poverty, and then there is the loss of independent lifestyle, as my colleague Königsberger-Ludwig has already mentioned. In this respect, it is right and good that the measures of the Federal Labour Market Disability Programme of the Social Ministry should take account of these spirals downwards. ParlaMint-AT_2009-07-10-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00032_d8e16406	Mr President. High house! Ladies and gentlemen! Front: Thank you very much to the representatives of the government parties for deciding here the Agricultural Law Change Act, which has been agreed. All those who have spent months giving lip recognition should help the Austrian dairy industry, and then do not do so at the decisive moment, must answer that – instead of making themselves here, moralizing and saying what you shouldn't have done. This law is a concrete help, it is a reasonable and wise solution to help the dairy industry in an extremely difficult situation. Thank you, therefore, for bringing this law into line here! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] In fact, we have been discussing this issue for a long time. It is undisputed that the situation is dramatic. The price situation in the dairy sector was very good a year and a half ago. Then there was an increase in production in Europe, and decisive markets are breaking away: the Chinese market – you remember the armored milk – and the Eastern European market. In addition, the European food industry – which is a crucial point – has pushed milk out of the recipe due to the high milk price, for example in the case of ice cream, but also in other areas. There about 30 to 40 percent of European milk has been processed in industry, and this milk is suddenly on the market. This is a development for which no one can do anything from the agricultural policy, which is simply market conditions. That is why we have been working from the beginning – I am from the Ministry, but also many political forces – to balance supply and demand, which is our priority goal. You know that the European Union has always refused to intervene in the market – this is self-regulatory, the approach. Therefore, the Commissioner for Agriculture was in Austria several times, and in Brussels too, it was urged that the EU intervene in the market. And we were successful: yes, she does! She's doing what she hasn't done so far. Intervention purchases – that is, buying butter and skimmed milk powder from the market, in stock; making export refunds – dairy products are sold out of the European Union to clear the market. We see that the EU needs to do more in this area. That is why I am proud that our initiative has been successful. You remember: At the beginning of this year, we wrote an Austrian milk memorandum, which was supported by Germany, Hungary, Slovenia and Slovakia. In the meantime, after months, it is also supported by France, and at the European level one speaks of the French-German-Austrian initiative for the dairy industry. We can be proud that we are among the three leading countries fighting for dairy farmers. Now, recently, one of our main demands was met, namely: The European Union will extend its intervention and export refunds. It would have run out in mid-August. The EU will extend it because it sees that milk and milk products are still too much on the European market – in order to relieve the market. This is a crucial point for us, I am very happy about it. We have also mentioned other things there: for example, that there should also be an export refund for cheese – that is not yet in the regime, but should also take place – as well as that the school milk supply will be extended. The children today do not only want to drink school milk, but also dairy products and, and,. The European Union is also in favour of this. So there are a lot of things we're going to do. In addition, the European Council, i.e. the Heads of Government, has dealt with the issue of milk. Our demand was also supported, which is that the European Union should in the past analyse the milk market in order to see if the quota phase-out makes sense. There are many, many things that are simply important, because we – this does not deny anyone – have a very, very difficult situation for the dairy industry. And that is why this Agricultural Law Change Act is important – because it gives me the opportunity to help dairy farmers at all! The dairy cow premium is a point. We have to go to 1. In August the European Union announced that we would also like to redeem the funds available there. And it was always a principle of Austrian policy as a whole and also of agricultural policy that, when funds are available in Brussels, we trigger this money, every euro and every cent. That's what I'm standing for! It is therefore important that we make the decision, because we can now inform the European Union: The funds, in about €12 million, we can trigger, will also cover them on the national side – that is important in this tense budget situation – and we will also receive co-financing from the countries so that we can pay the dairy cow premium to the dairy farmers. No one says that this alone is a blessing, but it is an important part of the support for the dairy industry! In addition, in the context of the Recovery Plan, there will be additional funds from unexploited agricultural resources, which will also be used to enable investment measures, pasture measures for Austrian dairy farmers. In total, this is a package of about €50 million that was promised by my predecessor at the time and is now becoming reality through this law. It is also important – and this has been mentioned by Mr Grillitsch – that I am now allowed by law to retain the one-percent increase in milk quotas from the health check that exists every year. I will also do so because in the milk sector, where we still have an oversupply, I do not want to stimulate production in addition. I also come to the legal situation, which allows me to tighten the balance. I'm against the abolition of salutation. And since you keep mentioning the key word of the “little farmer”: In about half of the Austrian dairy farmers delivered. There are large farms there, but also many, many small farms that deliver the milk quota. It is already a matter of doing with an eye to this and creating a balance, namely a balance between those who deliver it and those who deliver it. Those who, however, want to deliver extremely, so to speak, to use the system, should be asked more to cash out. That's what happens now, we can do it now, and that's important. Ladies and gentlemen, you must also bear in mind that the money paid by dairy farmers goes to Brussels. We can't use this nationally; we tried, but we can't. Therefore, it is not useful for us to abolish the salutation, but to tighten it up for those who use the system. That's what I'm standing for. Many, many other things are included in the Agricultural Law Change Act. This is absolutely necessary, but of course you are free to continue to examine the issue of constitutional law. [[Abg. Dr. Pirklhuber: Right: “many, many other things ”!]] Mr Pirklhuber! I'll tell you one thing: They make themselves here and talk about the small farmers and say that the funding is misdistributed. [[Abg. Dr. Pirklhuber: Yes, of course!]] – Do you know that on average the organic farmers are much larger than the conventional farmers? – They are far larger! And a sense of the environmental programme is to also get large-scale farms into the environmental programme – if a company suddenly loses any compensation, no premium, then it goes out of the programme – and then the sustainable, the ecological path we go to Austria makes sense. [[Abg. Dr. Pirklhuber: That's absurd what you argue!]] [[Abg. Dr. Pirklhuber: Now the organic farmers are already taken here to argue wrong agricultural policy!]] That's what I'm looking for. That makes us unique in Europe, that is right! But that's why it makes sense that even large-scale farms are involved here. If you mention industrial plants that receive premiums from the agricultural pot, this also makes sense: for example, because the European sugars take and do not buy cheap sugar on the world market. This is exactly the problem with milk: There is no such premium, and therefore the industrial plants buy vegetable fat from overseas, coconut fat, and use it for ice cream production. With regard to sugar, we still have such a system – and that is what is going on now – but that is why industrial businesses stand there and get a grant because they use European sugar. ParlaMint-AT_2009-07-10-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00032_d8e18037	We're coming to 10. Agenda item. There was no reporting. The first speech is from the honourable Member, Mr Ing. Hermann Schultes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-07-10-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00032_d8e18068	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! We have some laws relating to chemical substances today for treatment – later REACH; now it is about fluorinated greenhouse gases. There is a connection here because it concerns the implementation of EU directives and compliance with international agreements. Fluorinated greenhouse gases, such as gases that act like CO2, are environmentally harmful, they are fluorochlorocarbons or fluorinated hydrocarbons. They have a similar effect to nitrogen oxides, CO2, methane and sulphur hexafluoride, so they are also in this "liga" of dangerous gases. And these will be better monitored in the future, for which there will be appropriate training. The question is: Is it possible to prevent the release of these gases to the atmosphere? It does not seem to be very important, but I would like to point out that the European Union has taken over the obligation to reduce CO2 emissions or emissions of climate-damaging gases by around 330 million tonnes. REN. The contribution of these fluorinated greenhouse gases alone amounts to around 100 million tonnes of CO2 equivalents, possibly up to 2010. If we manage to achieve reductions in this critical area, then the pressure to reduce CO2 is not quite that large, although it remains large enough. That is why this complex law is important and important in terms of climate protection. Especially in these days, the major state-runners have once again agreed to take the issue of climate protection seriously. In 1997 the Kyoto Protocol was signed, as long as it has been, and there are still many who think: “Oh, God, we don’t have to take this so seriously! That's what someone's thinking. It may not be so important either.” I believe that there are still many in Austria who represent this opinion, unfortunately also in the various leading bodies. When I look at how the reactions to the Ecostrom Act are in Austria, I believe that there are still people in both the Chamber of Workers and in the Industrial Association who believe that climate change can be done in some way like the crisis and that then the world is beautiful again if you don't touch for as long as possible. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] At the moment we are experiencing that the crisis is being done as if many billions from the taxpayer cash could cover the crisis. I sometimes have the impression in this discussion – this is not actually taking place, but only blockades and emphasize the topic – that some believe that after the crisis – as you are already used to – high oil prices can also be offset by new oil price subsidies, because we have failed the way into the alternative forms of energy until then and will depend on oil at any price. I say at this point: I find this irrational, and I believe that in the near future we should increase the temperature of discussion a little, because those who have used the solidarity of the state very intensively in recent times in order to get over the crisis should also consider how we can live in Austria after the crisis with less dependence on supply and produce more energy in our own value creation. An urgent call. Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-07-10-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00032_d8e18129	Next speaker: Mrs Gessl-Ranftl. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-07-10-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00032_d8e18150	Mr President. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! As mentioned by Mr Schultes, the Kyoto Protocol regulates, in addition to greenhouse gases, carbon dioxide, methane and disticky oxide, a group of synthetic gases based on hydrocarbons, called fluorinated greenhouse gases or F-gases. The emission of this F-gase is much lower than that of the other group, but the impact on the greenhouse climate is much higher. The draft law submitted to us is now intended to serve the implementation and monitoring of the European F-Gase Regulation in Austria as well as the measures to fulfil Austria's Community obligations. The increased storms, which are now returning to our country, are already showing us how rapid climate change is actually going on. The most important measure in climate protection is the reduction of greenhouse gases, and I therefore see it as our all duty to try to bring this law into force as soon as possible. That this law will then be executed and the transgressions will be punished is certainly a matter of course. However, whether the relatively mild criminal provision definitely shows the desired deterrent effect is to be examined and, in my opinion, assimilation should be made if necessary. But this alone is still too little, but the public's environmental awareness should be made even more sensitive, with environmental awareness and behaviour mostly very far apart. Our task as a politician is to move forward with good example, because even small contributions, such as not buying spray boxes containing F-gase, are already showing a role model. We know that consumers in particular can, through their environmentally conscious purchasing behaviour, promote the production of environmentally friendly products. Ladies and gentlemen, a reduction in emissions of F-gases across the EU is, in my opinion, only the first step in the right direction in terms of climate change. As the greenhouse effect can already be felt worldwide, such laws would have to be carried beyond EU limits. I urge all of you to adopt the fluorinated greenhouse gas law 2009, which is truly of great urgency, as soon as possible. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2009-07-10-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00032_d8e18952	The motion for a resolution has been properly tabled and is under negotiation. The application reads as follows: [[Quoted print version removed]]
ParlaMint-AT_2009-07-10-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00032_d8e19022	Next speaker: Mr Hornek. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-07-10-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00032_d8e19044	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! Mrs Brunner, we have an excellent Minister for the Environment, and that is why your call is obsolete. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen! The European Union's chemical law has been well developed in recent years. Since there is a Community obligation, Austria has to re-adjust. The European Union's chemical law has been significantly developed and improved in recent years. The REACH Regulation regulates the officially supervised registration, evaluation, authorisation and restriction of chemicals and is based on industrial responsibility. Only substances registered may be placed on the market. Each manufacturer or importer shall have his own registration number for substances falling within the REACH Regulation. The assessment of these substances may also involve a restriction or authorisation procedure. In the case of restrictions, individual uses of the substance shall be prohibited. In the case of substances subject to authorisation, all uses of the substance shall be prohibited unless a specific application is granted. The purpose of the REACH Regulation is to strengthen the responsibility of the chemical industry and make it possible to identify and eliminate risks and risks of hazardous chemicals in the future through better data base. With the adoption of the REACH Regulation, the EU has taken an essential step in chemical policy for the safe handling of hazardous substances. The 1996 Chemicals Act is to be adapted to the REACH Regulation as a priority. This means that rules which do not comply with the European Union must be repealed and individual administrative simplifications should be achieved. To this end, It is to be said that the Austrian Chemicals Act of 1996 remains largely the case. The special feature of the REACH Regulation is the expansion of the communication of the supply chain. Users receive tasks and duties. For example, you must report information on the recycling of the substance to the manufacturer. This allows the manufacturer to recommend appropriate risk mitigation measures. The most important instrument in communication remains the safety data sheet. In the future, however, the registration number must be specified in addition. Where appropriate, information on the restriction or requirement to authorise shall be recorded on the sheet. Ladies and gentlemen! This proposal of the Chemicals Act is EU-compliant and serves to fulfil Austria's Community obligations. I ask you to give your consent.

ParlaMint-AT_2009-07-10-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00032_d8e20395	Mr Stauber. — (DE) Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I should like to speak on behalf of the Group of the I'll set the clock to 2 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-07-10-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00032_d8e20416	Madam President. Federal Minister! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Values listeners! In the European Union's environmental law, progress in European integration is very evident, as you have just mentioned. In particular, chemical law has been strongly developed in recent years and now includes additional, directly applicable regulations, for which there are no explicit legal accompanying rules in Austria. Therefore, it was also essential to adopt federal legislation in order to monitor and enforce the new regulations. In essence, the government template to be adopted today contains only implementing rules on directly applicable European legislation, which must at least be transposed into national law. The REACH Regulation and hence the so-called new European chemical policy have been applicable since 1. It shall apply directly as a Community regulation on June 2007 in the Member States. However, Member States must also define accompanying measures to monitor the provisions of the REACH Regulation and appropriate sanctions against infringements, which we are doing in the form of this law. The material most important part of the REACH Regulation is the provisions of Sections 1 to 3 on the registration of substances. In addition, in Sections 4 and 5, this Regulation also standards a number of obligations for suppliers of a supply chain, i.e. manufacturers, importers, dealers, professional processors and users of chemicals and mixtures. In principle, the REACH Regulation is aimed primarily at the professional, industrial and industrial sectors, not primarily at consumers, end-users and workers. Nevertheless, the REACH Regulation also contains some rules that are directly relevant to consumers and workers. It is worth mentioning and important here that employers have demonstrated to workers the information contained in the safety data sheet for all dangerous substances to which workers may be exposed. In addition, ECHA will also publish lists of particularly worrying substances on its website and provide on the Internet free of charge all information received via registration, non-confidential and electronically collected. All in all, then, this is an important legal regulation, which will also contribute to the safety of man and nature. - Thank you.

It is also welcome that the petitions and citizens' initiatives will no longer expire at the end of a legislative period, but will be assigned to the newly elected National Council if the deliberations are not yet completed. I think it is first of all a question of how we can develop petition law as an opportunity for dialogue between citizens and state, and secondly of the importance of the Committee as part of the parliamentary process. [[President Dr. Graf takes over the chairmanship.]] We have planned to submit joint proposals to the Committee on the Rules of Procedure in autumn. In Germany, for example, the right to petition was upgraded and was given a fixed place in the Basic Law. Since 2005, citizens have not only been able to address their concerns in writing, but have also been able to send them to the Committee by means of a web form via the Internet. Ten percent of the inputs are prepared in this form, and everyone can also discuss in a discussion forum. I think in this way it is also possible to organise better and more comfortable political participation via regional networking. These institutions in Germany could also serve as a model and guide for more citizens' proximity and participation. I am pleased that there is a willingness in the Committee to further improve and develop this dialogue between citizens and Parliament. We of the S & D Group will make us active for further development.

The requested opinion of the Federal Ministry of Transport states that the extension of the railway line is considered as part of the rolling continuation of the framework plan. The Federal Ministry notes that an expansion would be linked to benefits for local and regional transport as required by the contributors. This concerns how important and right the decision of our Federal Minister Doris Buresist to invest intensively in the infrastructure development of the railway so that such concerns can be implemented as soon as possible.

I therefore believe that it is the right approach to say that we also require two hours in road transport in the field of moped driver's license training in the future – and this also with a certain degree of attention, so that we say: Young people who want to be mobile should not be confronted with a training system that they cannot afford. We have therefore said: On the one hand, let's reduce a little bit the hours of theory and increase the hours of practice! Let us also create opportunities for this training to be done in the driving schools with two moped drivers, so that this is also manageable for young people so that they can be mobile. Our goal must be that they will be better trained in the future, so that we have fewer young people who may be unhappy with a moped accident. The third area, which is very important to me, where, according to all statistics, we have a negative development, is the safety of our children. If every fifth child sitting in the car today is unsecured – this is twice as much as a few years ago, which means a reduction in traffic safety and safety for our children –; if we know that for a child the risk of accidents in the car is greater than on the road – in the car, because they are not secured, more children than on the road or on the way to school – then, I think, there is a need for action! That is why I am pleased with all the measures we are taking: that adults who carry children in the car and do not secure them will receive coaching on the second time and will be informed about it – because they are often unaware of the dangers they pose to their children. It is important that they be informed of what it means if you don't pick up a child and put it in the car without a child's seat, and that this also has consequences. Mr Hagen, as executive officer, I would like to say the following: I find that at 80 km/h I'm running through the area where people are on the streets and believe that they're actually safe on the protection routes is highly unresponsible! If drivers with 80 km/h race through a local area, I don't think it's all right if you just as executive officials say it's too much if these people pay 70 € minimum penalty. I believe that this is the minimum we can demand in such a responsible behaviour as a legislator! [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] [[Abg. Hagen: I didn't talk about that either!]] With regard to the protection of our children in the car as well as children's seats, I would like to conclude by saying: The safety of our children is very important to me. We have prepared an information brochure. It has been possible with some federal states – and I am also seeking your support so that we can do this in all the federal states – to ensure that this information brochure is also provided with these baby packages, which a family receives at the birth of a child. This means that this part migrates from the infrastructure to the sales area. Here, further synergies can be created and savings can be achieved with the reduction of interface problems in the use of engines and personnel. This brings together drivers, motor vehicles, service, technical car service and the staff of the shift in a company. In particular in freight transport, the company, like the entire transport sector, struggles with collapses. But further liberalisation also brings new challenges. With your consent to this novel, give the company the opportunity to claim itself in a boundless Europe! Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2009-07-10-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00032_d8e27973	I now give the floor to the President of the Court of Auditors, Dr Moser. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-07-10-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00032_d8e27995	Mr President. High house! I should like to remind you that the report on the agenda today on the post-aeronautical surveillance aircraft Draken is the fifth in a long chain of audit reports from the Court of Auditors. I would like to remind you briefly that the first report dealt with the preparation of the successor procurement, the next concerned the type decision, the third was the signing of the purchase contracts, the follow-up report was the assessment and documentation of the counter-actions, and the report on the agenda today deals with the evaluation of the completed comparison and the subsequent change in the contract with Eurofighter GmbH. In this context, I would like to mention that the basis of these audits for the Court of Auditors was the constitutional and simple legal requirements, the requirements of the decisions of the National Council and military concepts. The Court of Auditors had to adopt and take note of these requirements as a condition for its conduct review. He had to examine whether and to what extent the legal and military requirements have been achieved in a economical, economic and appropriate manner and as far as possible tax-saving manner. This scale was also the subject of this examination concerning the comparison. The test has also shown that the comparison has resulted in a cost reduction of € 267 million – by ordering three airspace surveillance aircraft or operating equipment – or by achieving it. However, from the Court of Auditors' point of view, it is necessary to ensure, now, that the possible reductions in payments are envisaged, that the outstanding investment needs are concrete and that the military planning bases are harmonised and completed. In this context, the procurement process relating to the Jaguar weapon system should also be pointed out, which has caused a loss of effort in millions of heights – which has already been mentioned in the debate today. This procurement process has shown the basic problem of procurement in the Federal Ministry of Defence. In fact, it is true that the implementation measures lacked concrete planning tasks, content requirements, planning responsibilities and project structures as well as comprehensive cost assessments, both for procurement and for current operating costs. Needs assessments have not been carried out, priority series and concrete implementation periods have not been observed and no coordination has been made between investment measures in the areas concerned. These problems occur or occurred over and over again. This has also led to the need to stop and reverse procurement or to reprocess the procurement process. In general, it should also be noted that cost awareness – especially in planning – in the Federal Ministry of Defence is not very significant. The Court of Auditors therefore points out that these recommendations and shortcomings need to be offset as soon as possible, because it is important that the force can actually be provided with the necessary resources. However, this is not only a problem of the Federal Ministry of Defence; the examination of the ÖBB – concerning the X-ray scanner – has shown that there are the same problems there. A procurement without a demand survey, without taking into account the accompanying and follow-up costs, without operating scenarios and without involving the relevant stakeholders leads to significant damage, in this case of €3.8 million. [[Abg. May. Gaßner: There is no such thing in the churches!]] [[Abg. May. Kogler: When enough come together!]] The Court of Auditors would therefore expressly point out that it is necessary to actually implement the recommendations on the agenda today. I would therefore also like to ask you to take part in implementing these recommendations. Only in this way can funds be saved that we urgently need to meet the challenges of the future. Finally, I would like to thank you again for the good cooperation and wish you a nice holiday. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2009-07-10-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00032_d8e28955	We shall now proceed to the debate on the request made by Mr Strache, colleagues and colleagues concerning the establishment of a Committee of Inquiry pursuant to § 33 GOG-NR for a closer examination of political and legal responsibility in connection with the pioneering of Members and their employees or political officials by members of the Federal Ministry of the Interior, the Federal Ministry of Justice and the Federal Ministry of Defence and Sport and on the Proposal by Mr Cap, Head, Bucher, Dr. Pilz, colleagues, on the establishment of a Committee of Inquiry to examine hearing and influence measures in the field of Parliament. As already announced, applicants will first be justified by the two applications. The debate that followed will take place under one. Both requests have now been distributed to all MEPs. The two applications have the following general wording: [[Quoted print version removed]] Request by Mr Strache, Mr Vilimsky and other Members concerning the establishment of a Committee of Inquiry pursuant to § 33 GOG-NR for the further examination of political and legal responsibility in connection with the pioneering of representatives and their staff or political officers by members of the Federal Ministry of the Interior, the Federal Ministry of Justice and the Federal Ministry of State Defense and Sport The signed Members request that an investigation committee be set up in relation to SPÖ: 5, ÖVP: 5, FPÖ: 3, BZÖ: 2, GRÜNE: 2. Subject-matter of the investigation: Study of the political and legal responsibility of the Federal Minister for the Interior, the Federal Minister for Justice and the Federal Minister for State Defense. In this context, the Federal Ministry of the Interior, the Federal Ministry of Justice and the Federal Ministry of Defence and Sports have pioneered representatives and their staff or political officials, thereby bringing about legal violations, in particular, of the fundamental rights of delegates and their employees or political officials since 2004, as well as the Green Spittel and Data Abuse scandal. In particular, the following points should be considered: 1. Enlighten whether and for what purpose representatives, employees or political officials have been spyed out in the framework of the activities of the BIA or other federal institutions; 2. Infor­mation on the role of the BIA or other federal bodies, in order to avoid the legislation on immunity, as witnesses have been conducted and thus monitored; 3rd. Enlighten whether there has been politically motivated illegal investigations in recent years, avoiding the right of immunity; 4. Information on whether, in the context of the activities of the BIA or other federal institutions, there has been a legal non-covered surveillance measure, in particular against representatives, employees or political officials; 5. Information whether the Office for Internal Affairs (BIA) or other federal institutions have been abused for party policy purposes; 6. Information as to whether, within the framework of the prosecutor's orders to the security authorities, in particular the BIA, and the associated operations within the judicial department, undue differentiations have occurred in the course of the operation, depending on the person concerned; 7. Enlighten whether members of the BMI, BMJ and BMLVS have become unlawfully active against representatives, employees or political officials; 8. Enlighten whether several members of one or more federal ministries have politically motivated themselves into a group in order to pioneer representatives, employees or political officials; 9. Enlightenment whether and by whom members of the BMI, BMJ and BMLVS were instigated for abuse of office; 10. Enlighten whether and for what purpose representatives, employees or political officials have been spyed out by members of the BMI, BMJ and BMLVS; 11th@item: inlistbox Information on whether and for what purpose members of the BMI, BMJ and BMLVS have carried out an abuse of the infrastructure and logistics of a public service against representatives, employees or political functional research; 12. Information as to whether and for what purpose members of the BMI, BMJ and BMLVS have received compensation for monitoring or research; 13. Enlightenment to the extent to which political officials and their staff have been pioneered by members of the BMI, BMJ and the BMLVS in the Cabinet of the Federal Ministry of Defence and Sport; 14. Examination of legal and political responsibility in connection with the above-mentioned facts. Investigation order: The purpose of the investigation committee is to examine all legal and political responsibilities through the application of all the instruments provided for in the VO-UA to the subject matter of investigation and by taking into account all the files and other documents of the Federal Ministry of the Interior, the Federal Ministry of Justice and the Federal Ministry of Defence and Sport in connection with the subject matter of the investigation. Reasons: The monitoring and detachment of politicians and their environment was once the subject in the House and led, among other things, to the investigative committee on the cover-up of police apes and the abuse of political power, especially in the Federal Ministry of the Interior, but also in the Federal Ministries for Justice, for finance and for European and international affairs, which, however, in the autumn of the previous year, was still before the examination of the most important items of investigation due to the end of the legislative period. Today's day is not included in these figures, ladies and gentlemen – I did not want to prejudge the outcome of today's decisions with statistics. There were at least almost 3,000 written requests at this one session, and there were also 130 committee meetings and 32 subcommittee meetings. I think we can take a good look at these statistics, and I would like to stress this particularly because we should not make life difficult here ourselves, but also present our work to the public transparently. I see my job in making it possible, as President, to create a framework – as far as it is natural and not limited to me – to make it easier for you to work, to facilitate people's access, to facilitate transparency, to facilitate visibility. I think Austrian democracy needs that. Only a few weeks ago has the so-called value study been published. I have invited all of you to present this value study. Some of you have also come. One thing has particularly shaken me – and that was also the motivation to invite you – that the Parliament's reputation has fallen sharply over the last ten years, and we should work on it. And I think there's every and every one of us required. We can all make our contribution, especially in our dispute culture. I am often asked by visitors, of course I am also very often written, by e-mail or by letter: Why is it in the House's plenary as it is? – I try to present it again and again: This is parliamentaryism, which is democracy – the fight for the better arguments and the finding of compromises. There, where one no longer has any arguments, one has reached if it is an argument under the belt line. And this is in our hands that we are not here under the belt line, so to speak, to maintain the exchange, but with many, many good arguments let the better arguments penetrate and provide good solutions for the people, for the population.
